# **The comparison matrix** — which pairs of types compare at all — PostgreSQL 19beta1, 2026-09-10.
#
# `debts-v1.1.md` #43's second mechanism is `reconcile` retyping only literals, and its named
# symptom — `1::oid = 1::int8` — **does not reproduce today**: with #42's text-only fold both
# operands still fold to literals, so `reconcile` retypes them and the comparison answers. It is a
# latent defect, waiting for the fold to narrow further.
#
# So this matrix measures the surface the mechanism lives in instead: every ordered pair of the 52
# scalar types both sides have. The rows below are the **literal** form, which is what a capture can
# carry as a statement; the same 2,704 pairs were also measured over **columns**, and the two agree
# with each other on this oracle — which is itself the finding, because the node's two paths are
# different code and #43 is about exactly that difference.
#
# ## THINGS REASONING WOULD GET WRONG
#
#   * **`oid` and the `reg*` types compare with the integers and *not* with `numeric` or the
#     floats.** This node puts them in one "numbers" family, which was measured for the case it was
#     written for — `'text'::regtype = 25` against an uncast integer — and over-admits twelve pairs
#     PostgreSQL refuses.
#   * **`int2vector` and `oidvector` compare with nothing but themselves**, though they share
#     `text`'s representation here — nine more pairs the node answers and a real server refuses.
#   * **`citext` compares with the whole text family** and this node refuses it: ten pairs the
#     other way.
#   * `interval = time` answers on a real server.
#   * `lquery` has no equality at all, like `json` and `xml`.
#
# ## The measurements
#
SELECT ('x'::"char" = 'x'::"char") AS v	boolean
SELECT ('x'::"char" = '1'::bigint) AS v	!operator does not exist: "char" = bigint at character 21
SELECT ('x'::"char" = '1'::bit) AS v	!operator does not exist: "char" = bit at character 21
